## Document 010: 1802台玻 群益

### Page 1

產業速報  
                                        2026 年 10 月 21 日

1 群益投顧 
台玻 (1802 TT ) 10/20/2026 首戰台灣電路板產業國際博覽會 (TPCA Show 2026) 。本次
會 中 主 要 展 出 其 高 階 玻 纖 布 。 當 前 以 Low Dk1/Dk2 為 玻 纖 布 事 業 群 主 要 營 收來源。
Low CTE 則已於 2025 年 底 開 始 小 量 出 貨 ， 目 前 產 品 出 貨 量 已 較2026 年 初 顯 著 增 加 。
當前營收比重為 Low Dk2>Low Dk1=Low CTE 。鑒於 Low Dk 1/Dk2 產 品 出 貨 動 能 佳，
Low CTE 營 收 貢 獻 逐步提升下，毛利表現亦將持續走揚。產品供需結構尚處緊缺階
段， 公司 亦 有 望 部 分 反 映 報 價 。 給 予 投 資 建 議Buy ， 目 標 價 81 元。

研 究 員 ： 蔡 季 衡   
A52320@cim.capital.com.tw

台 玻 首 戰 TPCA Show ：  
台玻 10/20/2026 首戰 台 灣 電 路 板 產 業 國 際 博 覽 會(TPCA Show 2026) 。本次
會中 主 要 展 出 其 高 階 玻 纖 布 ， 包 括 TD(Low Dk1) 、 TDA(Low Dk2) 、 TS(Low 
CTE) 、 Q (Q 布 )等 ， 並 詳 解 各 產 品 特 性 及 運 用 範 疇 。 
一、 聚 焦 於 Low Dk 、 Low CTE 玻纖布 ：  
TD(Low Dk1) 、 TDA(Low Dk2) 具 低 介 電 損 耗 並 於 維 持 一 定 電 性 水 準 下 ， 提
供 較 佳 產 品 穩 定 性 。 目 前 主 要 應 用 於 電 子 產 業 及PCB 產 業 、IC 載 板 、 先 進
封 裝 材 料 、 航 空 及 汽 車 領 域 等 。 
TS(Low CTE) 具 低 熱 膨 脹 係 數 特 性， 並 具 備 高機 械 韌 性(剛性 )、 絕 佳 電 性 絕
緣 及 先 進 樹 酯兼 容 性。目 前 主 要 應 用 於 5G/6G 、微 波 通 訊 設 備、高 速 電 路 板、
高頻 PCB 、 光 模 塊 、 高 速 交 換 器 及資 料 傳 輸 等。  
二 、 目 前 營 收 比 重 為 Low Dk2>Low Dk1=Low CTE ：  
E 布 轉 至 中 國 產 銷 ，以 Low Dk 1/Dk2 為 當 前 玻 纖 布 事 業 群 主 要 營 收來 源 。
Low CTE 則 已於 2025 年 底 開 始 小 量 出 貨 ， 目 前產 品 出 貨 量 已 較 2026 年初
顯 著 增 加，預 期 營 收 佔 比 將 於 2027 年 陸 續 提 升，並 於 2028 年 伴隨 近 年 陸 續
擴建 之 新 產 能 開 出 後 ，顯 著 提 高 。鑒 於 目 前 下 游 終 端 需 求 仍 以 Low Dk 產品
為主力， 研究部 預期 2027 年 Low Dk2 營 收 比 重 仍 將 高 於 Low Dk1 及 Low 
CTE 。 當 前 營 收 比 重 為 Low Dk2>Low Dk1=Low CTE 。 另 外 ， 台 玻 目 前 亦嘗
試 生 產 測 試 Low Dk3( 三代布 )， 後 續 若 生 產 良 率 過 關 ， 亦 將送 樣 驗 證 。 
三 、 不 急 於 產 品 漲 價 ， 堅 守 優 質 客 戶 連 結：  
台 玻 生 產 研 發 玻 纖 布數 十 年，始 終 秉 持 於 維 繫 良 好 客 戶 關 係，並 以 客 戶 需 求
為 營 運 宗 旨 。 雖 2025~2026 年以來 E 布 報 價 倍 數 增 長 ， 惟 基 於多年來與 客
戶 合作 建立 之 信任 及當前產品利潤貢獻尚屬可控範圍，故而 預期將 不急於
大 幅 反 映 產 品 報 價 ， 但 亦 不 排 除 部 分 調 整 產 品 報 價 。 
四 、 具 備 Q 布 生 產 技 術 及 能 力：  
台 玻 於 本 次 TPCA Show 上 ， 已 展 出 Q 布 玻 纖 紗 ，顯示 其 Q 布 技 術 儲 備 ， 且
已 具 備 接 單 出 貨 能 力 。 惟 觀 察 AI 伺 服 器 規 格 調 整，預期 2027 年 產 品 需 求
仍以 Low Dk 系 列 玻 纖 布 為 主 ， 大 量 搭 載 Q 布 尚 早 。 不 同 材 料 規 格 之 玻 纖
布 ， 其 製 程 方 式雖 雷 同 ， 惟 配 方 均 有 差 異 ，窯 爐 使 用 亦 不 同 。 因 此 ， 台 玻 將
視 下 游 廠 商 產 品 需 求 ， 決 議 Q 布 生 產 時 程。

產業速報

2 群益投顧 
五、 研 究 部 預 估台玻 2027 年稅後 EPS 4.37 元 ：  
台玻 09/2026 營收 49.68 億 元 ，MoM+8.18% 、YoY+34.11% ，主因 Low Dk1/Low 
Dk2 等 高 階 玻 纖 布 出 貨 動 能 較 08/2026 提 升。若 以 玻 纖 布 營 收 比 重 40~45%，
且高階玻纖布佔其中 40~42% 推估， 09/2026 高階玻纖布營收貢獻約
MoM+7~9% 。 3Q26 營收 合計 約 141.41 億 元 ， QoQ+17.24% 、 YoY+31.82% ，
亦 優 於 原 預 期 之 138.80 億元， QoQ+15.08% 、 YoY+29.40% 。  
展望 2027 ~2028 年 ， 預 期 2027 年 Low Dk1/Dk2 玻 纖 布 出 貨 量 仍 將 較 2026
年 顯 著 提 升，Low CTE 營 收 貢 獻 亦 將 持 續 走 揚，2028 年 伴 隨 新 產 能 開 出 後，
有望趕上 Low Dk2 。 目 前 台 玻 Low CTE 產 品 已 陸 續 取 得 國 內 外 大 廠 認 證 ，
訂 單 能 見 度 亦 已 達 2027 年，且 2028 年 訂 單 洽 談 順 暢。因 此，供 應 鏈 產 品 需
求若 無 較 大 變 化 ，2028 年 玻 纖 布 事 業 群 仍 將持 續 成 長 。 
日本大廠 Nittobo 多 次 公 開 表 示 ， 雖 2028 年產能 倍增 ，惟 T 布 市 場 需 求 已
非 當 前 公 司 擴 產 速 度 所 能 消 化 ， 顯 示 產 品 需 求 強 勁 。 研 究 部預 估 台 玻 4Q26
底 ~2027 年 玻 纖 布 營 收 比 重 有 望 超 越 平 板 玻 璃 和 玻 璃 器 皿 。預估 2027 年營
收 649.42 億 元 ， YoY +26.21 %， 毛利率 32.01% ， 稅 後 淨 利 127.17 億 元 ，
YoY +90.06 %， 稅後 EPS 4.37 元 。  
投 資 建 議 ：  
台玻 10/20/2026 首 戰 台 灣 電 路 板 產 業 國 際 博 覽 會(TPCA Show 2026) 。本次
會 中 主 要 展 出 其 高 階 玻 纖 布。 當 前 以 Low Dk1/Dk2 為 玻 纖 布 事 業 群 主 要 營
收 來源。 Low CTE 則已於 2025 年 底 開 始 小 量 出 貨 ， 目 前 產 品 出 貨 量 已 較
2026 年 初 顯 著 增 加。 當 前 營 收 比 重 為 Low Dk2>Low Dk1=Low CTE 。 鑒於
Low Dk 1/Dk2 產 品 出 貨 動 能 佳 ，Low CTE 營 收 貢 獻 逐 步 提 升 下 ， 毛 利 表 現
亦 將 持 續 走 揚。產 品 供 需 結 構 尚 處 緊 缺 階 段，公 司 亦 有 望 部 分 反 映 報 價。給
予 投 資 建 議 Buy ， 目 標 價 81 元。

相 關 個 股 獲 利 預 估 、 評 價 及 投 資 建 議

### Page 3

產業速報  
                                        2026 年 10 月 21 日

3 群益投顧 
  【 投 資 評 等 說 明 】 
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy)  5%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無 法 由 基 本 面 給 予 投 資 評 等 
預 期 近 期 股 價 將 處 於 盤 整 
建 議 降 低 持 股

【 投 資 評 等 調 整 機 制 】 
  投 資 評 等 係「 首 次 」給 予 特 定 個 股 投 資 評 等 時，其 前 一 日 收 盤 價 相 較3 個月
目 標 價 之 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 計 算 而 得 。 個 股 投 資 評 等 分 為四 個 等 級 ， 定 義 如 上 。
爾 後 的 投 資 評 等係 依 循 「 首 次 評 等 」 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。  
「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」及 「 區 間 操 作 」均有 upside 目 標 價 。差 別 僅 在 於 ，
首 次 評 等 時 潛 在 報 酬 率 不 同 。 「 中 立 」 則 無 目 標 價 。 
一 旦 我 們 給 予 特 定 個 股「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」或「 區 間 操 作 」之 投 資 評 等，
就 是 責 任 的 開 始，爾 後 將 透 過 各 式 研 究 報 告 作 定 期 性、持 續 性 追 蹤 基 本 面 及
股 價 變 化 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。 
停 止 推 薦 情 境 ： 
1.  達 目 標 價 。 
2.  雖 未 達 原 訂 目 標 價 ， 但 檢 視 基 本 面 、 訊 息 面 、 籌 碼 面 等 多 方 訊 息 ， 研
判 股 價 上 漲 空 間 已 然 有 限 ， 將 適 時 出 具 降 評 報 告 。 
3.  推 薦 後 股 價 不 漲 反 跌 。

【免責聲明】  
  本 研 究 報 告 僅 提 供 予 特 定 人 之 客 戶 作 為 參 考 資 料「 非 經 同 意 不 得 轉 載 」 。我
們 並 不 確 保 此 資 訊 的 完 整 性 與 正 確 性，投 資 人 應 了 解，報 告 中 有 關 未 來 預 測
之 陳 述 可 能 不 會 實 現，因 而 不 應 被 依 賴。而 且 此 報 告 並 非 根 據 特 定 投 資 目 的
或 依 預 定 對 象 之 財 務 狀 況 所 撰 寫 出 來 的，因 此，此 研 究 報 告 的 目 的，既 非 對
投 資 人 於 買 賣 證 券、選 擇 權、期 貨 或 其 他 證 券 相 關 之 衍 生 性 商 品 提 供 詢 價 服
務，亦 非 作 為 進 行 交 易 的 要 約。投 資 人 應 注 意 到 相 關 證 券 之 價 值 及 收 益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

### Page 2

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 美國 9 月通膨數據高於市場預期，CPI 月增 0.6%、年增 3.7%；核心 CPI 月增
0.3%、年增 2.6%。同月 PPI 月增 1.3%，創 2022 年 3 月以來最大增幅，年增
6.3%， 顯示通膨壓力已逐步由能源價格擴散至更廣泛領域 。 數據公布後 ， 市場一
度提高對聯準會 10 月升息的預期，但隨後多位官員釋出審慎訊號，使升息押注
降溫 。 聯準會理事鮑曼表示 ， 決策前仍有時間觀察先前升息效果 ； 舊金山聯準銀
行總裁戴莉則認為 10 月無立即升息之必要。而褐皮書顯示，美國就業市場維持
「低招聘、低裁員」格局，而高能源價格帶來的通膨壓力仍值得關注。
⚫ 中國 9 月通膨持續回升，CPI 年增 0.9%，創近三個月高點；PPI 年增 5.0%，創
逾四年最大漲幅，顯示通縮壓力進一步減輕。主要受能源與原物料價格上漲帶
動，且 9 月進出口表現優於預期，AI 產業鏈需求與貿易環境改善提供支撐；不

過，信貸增長仍仰賴政府發債與季底效應，內需與民間融資動能仍偏弱。
⚫ 日本銀行審議委員小枝淳子表示 ， 不宜對升息過於保守 ， 並認為政策利率仍有進
一步上調空間。總裁植田和男則強調維持2%通膨目標的重要性。整體而言，日
本央行持續釋出偏鷹派訊號，有助支撐日圓表現。
⚫ 歐洲央行管委溫施表示 ， 在能源價格波動下 ，為了控制通膨仍不排除進一步升息
可能，總裁拉加德則認為法國債務問題應透過財政政策解決， 歐洲央行官員普遍
認為能源價格的潛在波動依然是推升通膨並使其居高不下的主要風險來源。。
⚫ 澳洲央行會議紀錄指出，能源價格上漲與需求韌性可能使通膨更具黏性，保留
進一步升息空間。然而 9 月就業數據意外疲弱，市場大幅下調升息預期，目前
預估 11 月維持利率不變的機率接近 90%。

＜上週走勢＞
上週台幣匯價高點為 31.890，低點在 32.520，以 32.338 作收，較前一周收盤價
貶值 4 角 6.3 分。
上週台幣走貶 ， 周初 ， 外資延續前周大幅匯出的走勢 ， 雖逢高吸引出口商拋匯 ，
仍不敵外資匯出力道令台幣走貶 ； 週二台股重挫近千點 ， 外資大幅賣超台股1653 億
並匯出，台幣接連跌破 32.000、32.100 及 32.200，總成交量 71.3 億，創歷史新高；
周三台股收漲，外資雙向進出，台幣收斂部分跌幅；週四，受美國通膨高於預期影
響，早盤台幣跳空開低，台股跌逾2%，外資大幅賣超 3025 億，台幣跌破 31.400 關
卡 ， 總成交量91.2 億 ， 再創歷史新高 ； 週五外資持續匯出 ， 台幣貶至周內低點32.520，
終場在央行調節之下收在 32.338。
＜未來分析＞
預估本週台幣走勢偏弱。就國際市場而言，美國 CPI、PPI 雙雙高於預期，通

膨壓力仍在，儘管聯準會官員陸續發言表示不急於升息，淡化市場對於聯準會十月
升息預期，惟年底前聯準會仍有升息一碼空間，利率持穩於高位將給予國際美元支
撐；就台灣市場來看，市場對AI 產業資本支出、融資成本及信用風險產生疑慮，若
賣壓延續，將壓抑市場風險偏好，增加外資調整台股部位及匯出的壓力，令台幣面
臨持續貶值的壓力，惟出口商拋匯將抑制台幣貶值速度，綜合以上，預估本週台幣
走勢偏弱。

美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Oct/19/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣震盪走升。上週人民幣中間價連續走升，週三中間價升至6.7249 創
三年八個月新高，惟市場靜待美國公布通膨數據，交投謹慎，人民幣僅小幅走升；
週三晚間，美國公布 9 月 CPI 年增率 3.7%、核心 CPI 年增 2.6%，雙雙高於預期，
帶動美元指數升至年內高點 102.945、 人民幣貶至周內低點6.7005； 然多位聯準會官
員發言表示聯準會不急於升息，令市場對於聯準會十月升息預期略有降溫，美元指
數自高點回落，激勵人民幣由貶轉升；臨近周末，聯準會主席華許於IMF 會議發言
並未指引十月聯準會政策利率走向，再度淡化市場對於聯準會十月升息預期，國際
美元走勢偏弱，人民幣走升觸及年內高點 6.6825，終場境外人民幣收在 6.6850，週
線升值 91 點，境內人民幣則收在 6.6870，週線升值 60 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣震盪偏升。就國際市場來看，美國仍面臨高通膨、年底可能將
再次升息，利差因素支撐美元指數於高檔震盪，限縮人民幣升值空間；就中國國內
市場來看，中間價持續偏升將帶動人民幣走升，加以出口商實質拋匯需求將支撐人
民幣表現；最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH 日線圖中，匯價維持在五日均線之下交
投 ， 均線呈現空頭排列 ， 加以USD/CNH 有效跌破 6.6900 整數關卡 ， 顯示USD/CNH
空方勢力較盛，綜合以上，預估本週人民幣震盪偏升。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6500-6.7000

美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Oct/19/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週 USD/JPY 先上後下。週初，受油價攀升推升美國通膨與利率預期帶動，美
元指數溫和走升創 6 月底以來新高，推升 USD/JPY 突破 158.50；週間，美國公布 9
月 CPI 高於預期，帶動美元指數升至 2025 年 5 月以來高點，也使 USD/JPY 最高來
到 158.94，然逢高獲利了結賣壓出籠使匯價回落；臨近週末，USD/JPY 一度下探至
157.53，隨後逢低買盤進場收復部分跌幅，終場收在 157.99，週線下跌 13 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、受美伊戰事推升汽柴油飆漲
與服務成本擴散影響，美國 9 月 CPI 及 PPI 月增漲幅均超出市場預期，加上初請失
業金人數連五週低於 20 萬人顯示勞動市場穩定，市場預期聯準會將於 12 月再次升
息，美日實質利差維持高檔，持續為USD/JPY 提供下檔支撐。二、被視為鴿派的日
本經濟財政大臣城內實明確表態，貨幣政策應交由日本央行自行判斷，並宣告日本
已擺脫長期通縮、轉型為增長導向經濟體。此番言論降低政治干預日銀進一步升息
的疑慮，在貨幣政策正常化的腳步下，預計將限縮 USD/JPY 的上行空間。三、就技
術面而言，日線圖中，USD/JPY 匯價目前交投於月線及年線之間，各天期均線高度
糾結，多空雙方於 158 關卡附近拉鋸，在有效突破季線及年線反壓或跌破月線支撐
前 ， 仍以區間震盪看待。 綜合以上 ， 預估USD/JPY 本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較高 。

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週 EUR/USD 震盪走升。週初，持續受法國財政赤字與歐債利差擔憂拖累，
EUR/USD 震盪走弱；週間，因美國9 月 CPI 高於預期，市場重新評估聯準會年底前
升息腳步，導致 EUR/USD 跳水最低來到 1.1112；臨近週末，儘管美國 PPI 與零售
銷售數據偏熱，市場優先反映美債殖利率回落與風險資產重新定價，美元多頭獲利
了結帶動 EUR/USD 谷底反彈 ， 最高來到1.1269， 終場收在1.1239， 週線上漲42 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 區間偏空震盪，主要原因如下：一、法國央行月度展望報
告指出，法國第三季 GDP 預估僅微幅成長 0.1%，印證近期高能源的成本已實質拖
累歐元區核心成員國增長。同時，歐洲央行總裁拉加德明確拒絕出手干預法債，要
求法國透過自身財政政策化解赤字困境；在明年法國大選前的財政隱憂與基本面疲
弱下，預計將令歐元上檔持續承壓。二、儘管歐洲央行管委溫施與總裁拉加德保留
12 月升息預期，加上德國基建與軍費支出為歐元提供部分下檔保護，但市場穩固預
期聯準會年底前將再升息一次。在美國經濟韌性與高利差優勢主導下，預計將限縮
歐元反彈空間。三、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD 自低點反彈並站回五日線
之上，然而中長期均線下彎仍維持空頭排列，RSI 指標亦處於 50 中軸下方，顯示整
體空方趨勢尚未扭轉，反彈至月線附近恐面臨較大賣壓。綜合以上，預估EUR/USD
本週呈現區間偏空震盪的可能性較高。

                           新台幣利率走勢分析

115. 10.12-115.10.16 
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.805% ~ 0.830%  
30 天期短票利率   1.67% ~ 1.68% 
公債－10 年期-A15109     2.020~2.025% 
－ 5 年期- A15108 1.88%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分 ，本週央行連續減發定存單使市場資金寬鬆 ， 雖外資大舉匯
出，資金供給仍充裕，帶動短率朝區間低位移動。 
週五央行標售 2 年期定存單 250 億元 ， 加權平均得標利率為1.58%， 低於市場
預期區間 1.60%-1.62%，較上期標售結果下跌 5.8 個基本點。 
週 五 隔 夜 拆 款 利 率 區 間 在 0.805%~0.830% ， 30 天 期 短 票 主 要 成 交 在
1.67%~1.68%，利率大致持平於上週。 
二、 5 年期指標券 A15108 週二搓合成交在 1.88%，後隨美債利率滑落，加上本週
2 年期公債標債結果提振市場持券信心，帶動 10 年期指標券 A15109 週四向
下成交在 2.02%~2.025%。 
在銀行去化短期資金及補充 LCR 之需求下 ， 週三2 年期公債得標利率為1.65%，
落在市場預估區間下緣 ， 投標倍數回升至2.86 倍 ， 銀行業得標比重上升至88%。
股市承壓帶動避險需求，美債利率大幅回檔，促使台債交投偏空氛圍轉趨和
緩 ， 惟在央行維持緊縮貨幣政策下 ， 買盤積極追價的意願不強 ， 預期短線台債
殖利率呈區間格局的可能性較高，關注下週 10 年券標售結果，預估 5 年券區
間為 1.85%~1.90%，10 年券區間為 1.98%~2.03%。

### Page 1

ab 15 October 2026
Global Research
First Read
Genius Electronic Optical
Private placement with 3M expands its optical
connectivity opportunity
Genius approved a private placement with 3M
On 15th Oct, Genius's board approved a private placement to 3M UK Limited for
5.54mn shares at NT$869.6 per share, an 18.3% discount to the 14th Oct closing price
NT$1,065, representing NT$4.8bn investment and 4.68% stake in Genius. Proceeds
from the transaction will fund technology development, working capital, and bank loan

repayment. The two companies also announced a strategic partnership to develop
optical communication for AI data center and HPC. We believe this announcement is a
positive validation for Genius working with a tier one supplier in optical connectivity to
provide its precision components for advanced optics.
3M EBO technology could open up a US$100mn TAM for Genius
3M has been developing its EBO (expanded beam optical) technology since 2019 with
commercialization in late 2024 and deployment for Microsoft Azure data centers in July

2026. EBO is an upgrade to MPO connectors at the ends of fiber optics cables.  The
EBO is a lensed, non-contact ferrule that uses a prism lens to expand and collimate a
light beam to send across fiber before refocusing into a receiving fiber, improving dust
tolerance to cut cleaning steps and cable inspection times 85%. The technology was
supported by the MSA launched in May 2026, co-chaired by Oracle, with Meta, Senko
and Molex among the members for scale-out cabling. We saw opportunity opened for

Genius on the EBO ferrule, a small molded plastic part with a row of micro-lenses to
widen the light beam plus an anti reflection coating. This could open up a US$100mn
market TAM for Genius based on 4-12 ferrules per GPU (2-3 connections x 2-4 plugs per
connection) x 20mn accelerators x US$1 for the lens assembly.
Validation for its optical solution push with potential hyperscaler exposure
We believe the scale of placement at NT$4.8bn suggests scope to expand into CPO

where it could improve the MT ferrule used as the detachable plug end of the FAU to
hold the fiber in position when it is connected to a switch adapter or removable cable.
Genius  collaboration with 3M can bring the company closer to the end demand with its
direct partnership with Microsoft and bring revenue contribution forward to 2027,
ahead of Genius CPO ramp in 2028E. For CPO, we still see potential to reach 10% of the
TAM supplying MT ferrule, prism and V-groove.

Reiterate Buy with PT at NT$1,250
Our recent update on Genius had raised the PT from NT$700 to NT$1,250 reflecting the
company's potential CPO market with its progress sampling three precision components
inside the fiber array unit (FAU) targeting FAU integrators such as Coherent and FOCI.
The collaboration strengthens our confidence on Genius capability within the optical
connectivity industry, and reiterate our Buy on the compony.

Company Description
Founded in 1989, Genius Electronic Optical (GSEO) is a leading global manufacturer of
camera lenses, providing full service optical solutions, including mobile lenses, automotive
lenses and AR/VR optics. Its production sites are in Taichung (one plant) and Xiamen (two

plants). The Taichung plant mainly manufactures VR lenses, while the Xiamen plants produce
compact lenses for consumer electronics. Optical lens products accounted for 73% of 2023
total revenue, and others include TOF lenses, flash lenses, VR lenses and LED lighting
products. Its major client is Apple and it supplies lenses for iPhones and iPads.
UBS EPS and Consensus EPS
The UBS EPS is an adjusted diluted EPS metric. It is calculated using the UBS analyst's

interpretation of earnings suitable for valuation purposes divided by the diluted number of
shares. This may differ to the way the consensus EPS metric has been calculated.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
Our price target is based on PE-based sum-of-the-parts model.
Genius' growth is highly leveraged to the overall handset industry and could be affected by
volatility in handset demand, as well as whether handset OEMs will continue upgrading their

camera design. Genius' margins could be affected by increasing competition and the rise of
new technologies, ASP pressure from customers and fluctuating material and labour costs.
Downside risks include competitors catching up in technology, yield rate issues for new
products and uncertain iPhone demand.

KEY DEFINITIONS:Forecast Stock Return (FSR) is defined as expected percentage price appreciation plus gross dividend yield over
the next 12 months. In some cases, this yield may be based on accrued dividends. Market Return Assumption (MRA) is defined as
the one-year local market interest rate plus 5% (a proxy for, and not a forecast of, the equity risk premium). Under Review (UR)
Stocks may be flagged as UR by the analyst, indicating that the stock's price target and/or rating are subject to possible change in the

near term, usually in response to an event that may affect the investment case or valuation. Equity Price Targets have an investment
horizon of 12 months.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

considerations may be lower or higher than portfolios where ESG factors, exclusions, or other sustainability issues are not considered by the portfolio manager, and the

## Document 126: 261019_citi_dynapack

### Page 1

Improving 3Q GPM helped by better mix — Dynapack's preliminary 3Q26 results 
showed margin recovery yet weak PC sales remained the key drag on the earnings. 
3Q26 EPS of NT$2.33 (+72% q/q and +49% y/y) missed our/BBG est by 45%/27%, 
although we believe the earnings shortfall was well anticipated given the 3Q sales 
miss owing to the delayed shipment of one of the BBU products and weaker PC 
demand. GPM improved q/q on a richer product mix, with higher-margin BBU 
making up c.50% of 3Q sales on our estimate, while OPM recovered to 10.7%, 
+4.2ppt q/q and +1.3ppt y/y. Nevertheless, the sales shortfall constrained op 
leverage, leaving margin improvement insufficient to offset weaker sales. 
PSU vendor shortages weigh on Oct; 12kW ramp to drive Nov sales rebound — 
Our industry checks suggest that Dynapack's major PSU vendor has encountered 
component supply shortages (related to power management IC) and therefore 
delayed BBU pull-ins, weighing on shipments of Dynapack's existing 3kW and 5kW 
BBU products in Oct. We therefore expect Oct-26 BBU sales to decline slightly m/m. 
Meanwhile, PC sales should recover sequentially and more than to offset the BBU 
softness, supporting a slight m/m increase in group sales. Looking beyond Oct-26, 
we expect accelerating shipments of the new 12kW BBU series from Nov-26 to drive 
a strong rebound in BBU sales.  
Buy 
Catalyst Watch: Upside, expires 26-NOV-26

Implications — We reiterate our bullish stance on the BBU sector despite near-term
volatility from external supply-chain constraints, which is likely to put pressure on
near-term sentiment. We view the component-driven delays in product pull-ins
primarily as a shipment-timing issue, with underlying demand remaining strong into
2027E. We also see ample opportunity for Dynapack to gain market share from

Panasonic, its major Japanese peer, particularly as power architectures migrate
toward HVDC. This share-gain potential remains intact, reinforcing our confidence
in Dynapack's medium term BBU growth outlook.

Dynapack International (3211.TWO)
19 October 2026 Citi Research

Dynapack International
Valuation
We assign a 32x target P/E to our 2027E EPS of NT$25 to derive a target price of NT$800. We value Dynapack based on 2027E
target P/E as we believe 2027E better reflects its earnings power after BBU capacity expansion. Our target P/E of 32x is based
on our forecast EPS CAGR of 66% in 2025-2027E and a PEG of 0.5x, which we benchmark against its major PSU partner, as we

expect Dynapack to grow alongside its partner.
Risks
Our quantitative model assigns a High Risk rating to Dynapack. However, we believe such a rating is not warranted for
Dynapack due to: 1) the company is generating solid revenue and earnings growth over next 3 years; the underlying industry,
BBU for AIDC, also enjoys secular growth; 2) the business model that involves with product customization and strict
safety/reliability requirements would imply relatively benign competition and therefore stable pricing and margins; and 3) the

company has a strong balance sheet and has been in net cash position since 2019.
Key downside risks to our price target/rating are: 1) Slower-than-expected BBU adoption in AIDC, 2) Delay in PSU customer
qualifications, 3) Competition from larger battery or power solution vendors, 4) Safety, certification and reliability issues.
Panasonic Holdings
(6752.T; ¥3957.0; 1; 16 Oct 26; 15:30)
Valuation
To derive our target price, we use a global sum-of-the-parts (SoTP) approach. In the case of Panasonic Holdings, we reference

52 other companies in the Citi coverage universe to set a fair value PER for each business division, which we sum to derive our
overall target price.
Panasonic fair-value PERs for individual business segments range from 8x to 31x, and have a weighted average of 26.2x
(excluding subsidies in the battery business), taking minority interest and equity in earnings into account. We estimate
business-derived equity value of ¥5,000/share. We calculate the present value of the amount we expect Panasonic to receive

in battery subsidies over five years at ¥400/share. Combining these two values, we derive a target price of ¥5,400.
Risks
Risks to our target price include 1) unsuccessful management policies or strategies resulting in weaker-than-expected
business investment returns or sales growth, or conversely producing greater-than-expected results at a relatively early stage,
2) stagnation in the global economy or an upturn in it, and rises or declines in demand in the electrical industry, and 3) forex

trends. If these factors manifest themselves differently than we have anticipated, the share price may vary from our target
price.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

營收走勢圖

研究員/張均壕 JUN_HAO1@uni-psg.com

研究部仍看好時碩未來營運，主要是考量液冷散熱零件業務隨著客
戶訂單逐步放量及出貨規模擴大 ，帶動 2H26 業績大幅提升。 此外 ，
航太事業成長前景仍佳，工業事業亦可望持續增長，明年展望依舊
正向。研究部預估時碩 2026 年 EPS 3.52 元(+162%YoY)，2027 年
EPS 4.63 元(+32%YoY)。以明年獲利為評價基礎，給予 20 倍本益
比，目標價 93 元。

重點摘要：

1. 受惠AI趨勢，液冷散熱訂單將帶動3Q26業績強勁增長： 
時碩3Q26合併營收上升至18.18億元(+24%QoQ，+43%YoY)，主
要是受惠AI資料中心液冷相關零組件的貢獻增加，令7、8、9
月單月營收持續創新高 。考量營收擴大， 規模效應顯現， 預期
時碩3Q26毛利率將提升至 24.5%，營益率提升至 8.8%，預估
3Q26稅後淨利1.38億元(+101%QoQ，+34%YoY)，預估3Q26EPS 
1.26元。

2. 液冷散熱產品進入爬坡期，4Q26營運淡季不淡： 
展望4Q26， 雖然年底通常為時碩汽車、工業業務的淡季， 但受
惠散熱零件仍處在出貨爬坡期，預期季度營收與獲利仍有望
再墊高。研究部預估時碩 4Q26合併營收18.85億元(+4%QoQ，
+55%YoY)，4Q26稅後淨利1.40億元(+1%QoQ，+113%YoY)， 預估
4Q26EPS 1.27元 。 全年度來看 ， 研究部預估時碩2026年合併營
收將提升至65.25億元(+31%YoY)，年度毛利率24.1%，營益率
7.1%，稅後淨利3.87億元(+161%YoY)，預估2026年EPS 3.52
元。

3. 航太業務維持成長動能，積極拓展新商機 
受惠於全球航太產業需求穩健及上游關鍵零組件產能吃緊，
時碩管理層持續看好航太業務前景 ，其中天陽航太營運成長
率預估可達15%～20%。 此外 ， 公司積極擴充泰國航太產能 ， 規
劃建置鋁合金及不鏽鋼生產線，以掌握全球航太供應鏈重組
及非紅供應鏈商機 ， 中長期目標為貢獻約10億元營收 。 除既有
航太業務外，時碩亦積極拓展人形機器人及半導體設備等新
興應用領域。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

在人形機器人方面， 時碩已投入諧波減速器及關節零組件開
發，並與國際及中國大陸主要廠商展開合作。半導體設備方
面，時碩持續開發相關精密零組件，未來將配合泰國新廠布
局 ， 爭取台灣客戶海外工廠的供應商機 。 整體而言 ， 隨著航太
業務穩健成長，以及人形機器人與半導體設備等新領域逐步
拓展 ， 將有助於公司建立更多元的營運成長動能 ， 降低對傳統
汽車產業的依賴。

4. 明年業績續升，預估2027年EPS 4.63元： 
展望2027年 ， 考量日系大廠的液冷散熱訂單將延續至明年 ， 而
且還有美系散熱大廠客戶訂單與磁浮壓縮機關鍵訂單等待發
酵 ， 加上航太業務仍具偏強增速 ， 預期時碩2027年業績將持續
提升。研究部預估時碩2027年合併營收74.20億元(+14%YoY)，
年度毛利率 25.0%， 預估2027年EPS將進一步提升至 4.63元。

附錄 一 ： 評 等 之 標 準 
評等  定義  
強力買進     預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬超過 25%以上  
買進  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬 10% 以上  
中立  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 10～-10%  
降低持股  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 (-10%)以下  
未評等  沒有足夠的基本資料判斷該公司評等

研 究  報 告                
樺漢(6414.TT) 
產業：電子–電腦及週邊設備
3Q26 營收季增 3%，4Q26 營收預估與 3Q26 相當 
 投資評價與建議 
未來營收成⻑端視北美建廠需求與AI ， 毛利率提升主要仰賴整併Kontron的綜
效：樺漢計畫拿下3億美元之實體AI市佔，工控物聯網成⻑將由邊緣AI帶動，
北美建廠需求使智慧工廠持續成⻑，毛利率則藉由產品組合調整、新服務模式
的滲透率與整併Kontron的綜效以向上提升。 
 重點評論分析 
3Q26營收季增3%(年增48%) ， 智慧工廠3Q26營收季減2%(年增84%) ：3Q26
營收496.4億元季增3%(年增48%)符合預估值(496.5億元)，工業物聯網事業
群、智慧軟體與方案事業群及智慧工廠與廠務事業群，分別季增4%、9%及-
2%(年增44%、-9%及84%)，智慧軟體與方案事業群4Q26為政府及企業單位
結帳高峰，4Q26營收比重可望高於3Q26，有助提升毛利率。 
4Q26營收預估與3Q26相當，在手訂單達2,550億元以上： 4Q26營收預估與
3Q26相當，成⻑驅動主要來⾃AI應用擴展 （由邊緣運算走向智慧平台）、半導
體擴產（先進製程/封裝/AI基建） 、智慧製造升級（⾃動化/機器人/數位轉型）
及全球在地化供應鏈布局；2,550億元以上的在手訂單能見度已延伸至 2027

樺漢看好實體AI：過去幾年全球聚焦於生成式AI，讓AI進入大眾生活與文字對話，樺漢認為生成式AI僅是起點，下⼀波
產業革命的關鍵在於實體AI，將讓AI開始進入真實世界，具備『看得見、聽得懂、想得通、做得到』的感知、理解、決
策與⾃主行動能力；根據Next Move Strategy Consulting預估，全球實體AI市場規模將由2025年的58 億美元成⻑至
2035年的1,200 億美元(CAGR35%)，10年預估成⻑將超過20倍；目前樺漢已全球部署超過100萬套系統，包含
Casino/Gaming (7.5萬/21萬套)、Lottery (6萬套)、ATM (25萬套)及POS/Self-Checkout (35萬套)，實體節點分布極為
廣泛；樺漢已打造⼀套共通核⼼技術平台，涵蓋Compute Platform (SOM/Edge)、Sensor Suite (Camera/LiDAR/IMU)
及Drive & Actuator、Connectivity (5G/Wi-Fi/TSN)，同⼀套平台可靈活⽀援 AMR（⾃主 移動機器人）、Mobile 
Manipulator （移動操作機器人）、Humanoid （人形機器人） 及Industrial Robot （工業機器人），大幅降低研發成本，
加速產線規模化複製落地 ； 樺漢機器人團隊已完成核⼼技術平台整合 。 早期 AMR 與工業⾃動化機器人已在智慧工廠與
物流場域大量量產；針對具備感知能力的人形機器人 （Humanoid）與 Mobile Manipulator，公司透過 NVIDIA Isaac
數位雙生進行驗證，預計於2026年起陸續展開客戶PoC與場域導入，並配合 Kontron 歐美通路擴大推廣，目標在10年
內拿下3億美元之實體AI市佔。

| 樺漢的定位與優勢 
樺漢是鴻海艦隊的工業電腦旗艦：樺漢科技（6414）是鴻海集團旗下的重要工業電腦（IPC）與工業物聯網廠，是鴻海
艦隊的工業電腦旗艦。鴻海透過旗下投資公司共持有樺漢約24%的股權，為其最大股東與⺟集團，雙方在資源、供應鏈
及高科技AI智慧製造上緊密合作。樺漢的產品應用領域非常多元，包括智慧零售、智慧監控、智慧家居、資訊安全、交
通、製造、能源、醫療、建築、園區等，且透過整合硬體、軟體與人工智慧驅動服務積極推進ESaaS(樺漢解決方案即服
務)計畫，以滿足客戶多樣化需求。 
三大事業群架構分別為工業物聯網、智慧軟體與方案及智慧工廠與廠務：樺漢的三大事業群架構分別為工業物聯網事業
群、智慧軟體與方案事業群及智慧工廠與廠務事業群；工業物聯網事業群包括邊緣運算、網安通訊、工業與嵌入式系統
整合 。 智慧軟體與方案事業群著重高毛利軟硬雲整合 ， 涵蓋工業軟體 、 機器學習 、 數位雙生 、 雲地平台系統整合 ， Kontron 
在此板塊扮演關鍵的歐洲市場與軟體加值核⼼。智慧工廠與廠務事業群提供半導體設備及廠務系統、智能系統整合、高
科技材料與設備，由帆宣(6196)主導。 
併購帶來營收規模快速成⻑，三大事業群各有所⻑：樺漢透過併購壯大規模，營收由2015年的111億元僅短短十年就快
速10倍成⻑至2025年的1,423億元 ， 尤其入股Kontron(8/20後樺漢持股49.4%)與帆宣更是關鍵 。 樺漢看中Kontron的品
牌加持與歐洲的高市佔率，目前軌道交通產品Kontron已掌握歐洲逾50%市佔，並著手把相關方案導入亞太市場。樺漢
和Kontron在技術合作方面，建立共享平台，全面整合研發、製造、供應鏈、IT和人才等資源，協同設計產品，建構具
有全球競爭力的製造系統；產品整合則共享全球品牌資源，提升市場認知度與價值定位，並深入推廣產品線至亞太、歐
洲、北美等全球市場中⼼；市場聚焦方面，結合Kontron在基礎作業系統(Kontron OS)、物聯網軟體和5G網通方面的軟

實力，以及樺漢在邊緣AI伺服器、機器人和強固型工業電腦方面的硬體優勢，共同打造實體AI技術平台。 
樺漢併購帶來營收規模快速成⻑

資料來源 : CMoney，玉山投顧整理、繪製 
實體 AI 帶來新⼀輪商機，樺漢拚五年內全球工業電腦第⼀名：實體 AI 的應用廣泛，如⾃動搬運叉車可應用於倉儲及
工廠內物料搬運，具備即時導航與避障，可提升效率並降低人力依賴。服務型機器人則可應用於清潔、導引及巡檢，
具有穩定感知與定位，可適用商辦、醫院、公共空間。樺漢認為，生成式 AI 只是開始。下⼀個十年真正改變世界的，
不只是 AI 會回答問題，而是 AI 開始進入真實世界。逐漸開始看得見、聽得懂、想得通、做得到。實體 AI 的定義應該
是讓 AI 具備感知、理解、決策與行動能力。樺漢預估 2035 年的實體 AI 市場規模可達 1,200 億美元，樺漢欲藉此商
機於五年內攻佔全球工業電腦第⼀名。樺漢目前已透過旗下全資⼦公司超恩，強化邊緣運算產品線，並結合 NVIDIA
的 Nemotron 模型，處理影像、聲音與文字等多模態資料。這類架構把資料分析留在終端端點執行，對應即時反應與
隱私要求較高的場域，也減少雲端端 AI 運算負擔。樺漢同步延伸 ESaaS(Ennoconn Solution as a Service，樺漢即服
務)服務內容，將硬體、軟體與人工智慧驅動服務整合到同⼀套方案中。樺漢 AI 聚焦在應用層與電力能源層，應用在智
慧製造、智慧城市、ESG 以及發展利基小語言模型。

Kontron 的綜效而定。

| 工業電腦產業簡介 
工業電腦對環境的適應力高於個人電腦：1984年，IBM發表了5531型工業電腦，隨後於1985年推出基於Intel 80286處
理器的IBM7531，正式開啟工業電腦新紀元。工業電腦（IPC）發展至今已有40多年的歷史，從最初只負責簡單的流程
監測，進化到現今具備高效能算力的邊緣控制中⼼，其最大的特⾊在於使用「工業級用料」，能夠保障⻑達10至 15年
的使用需求，並且能克服各式嚴苛的環境挑戰，確保設備能持續穩定地運作。工業電腦最顯著的特性便是其針對惡劣環
境的耐用性，為了應對容易有大量粉塵的環境，多數的機型都採用「無風扇」和「⼀體成形」的設計，避免風扇故障引
起的熱當機或灰塵吸入造成的短路；此外，為因應戶外作業或極端氣候，大部分的工業電腦都會通過寬溫(例如：可運行
的溫度區間為 -40°C～85°C)與寬壓(例如：可輸入電壓區間為 9V～36V)測試，並搭載防塵、防水、防震、耐衝擊的機
制，確保設備在震動頻繁的環境下仍能穩定運作，工業電腦要具備嚴苛環境的適應力，高可靠度，擁有豐富的工業接口
及較⻑的生命週期管理。 
工業電腦與⼀般電腦的差異

資料來源 : Google、 玉山投顧整理、編製

### Page 7

7 
工作溫度 、 防護等級等為評定工業電腦優劣的重要指標 ， 以冷卻機制來說 ， 個人電腦依賴主動散熱 ， 灰塵與故障風險高 ，
工業電腦則採被動、密封設計，降低⻑期失效機率；擴充與客製化方面，工業電腦可高度客製，對應工業、醫療、車載
等垂直場域需求，而非僅僅只有通用規格；電源管理能力方面，工業電腦具備抗突波與點火控制，能在不穩定供電下持
續運作；至於作業系統穩定性方面，工業電腦多採用⻑期⽀援版本，可避免更新中斷營運。 
工業電腦優劣的重要指標

資料來源 : Google、 玉山投顧整理、編製  
工業電腦的應用場域多元化：隨著技術的演進，工業電腦的應用場景早已跨越工廠的圍牆，在各個專業領域的解決方案
成功落地，從車載應用、軌道交通、船舶海事、無人載具、人型機器人、工業機器人、工業⾃動化、5G通訊網路到邊境
安防，工業電腦的應用場域多元化，隨處可見。 
工業電腦的分類與應用場景

資料來源 : Google，玉山投顧整理、編製 
⾃從工業電腦從工廠走進我們生活日常的POS、交通售票系統、醫療診斷到博弈娛樂等，它的重要性就日益加重，舉例
來說 ， 於智慧醫療其應用於影像診斷與手術輔助 ， 核⼼在於精準度與醫療級電氣安全 ， 錯誤的成本極高 ； 智慧交通方面 ，
車載與軌道監控需⻑時間運算與抗環境⼲擾，任何中斷都直接影響公共安全。 
全球工業電腦市場2035年將達104億美元，工業電腦台廠各有專精：根據研調機構構Research Nester統計與預估，全
球工業電腦市場規模將由2025年的61億美元，成⻑至2035年的104億美元，年均複合成⻑率為7.9%，台廠從上游半導
體至下游電⼦組裝產業供應鏈完整，具上下游垂直整合優勢且各有專精。 
全球工業電腦市場2035年將達104億美元

資料來源 : Research Nester，玉山投顧整理、繪製

### Page 8

8 
台廠具上下游垂直整合優勢

資料來源 : Google，玉山投顧整理  
AI時代帶來邊緣運算需求 
AI時代到來，工業電腦將從硬體代工走向邊緣運算平台化，軟體的整合能力將決定產品毛利率的高低，工業電腦也從第
⼀代的控制功能跨步到第⼆代的連網功能，再進而邁向大步往第三代的人工智慧及時推理功能前進。未來，工業電腦已
不再只是單純的控制設備，而是逐步成為集資料蒐集、即時運算與通訊於⼀⾝的邊緣中樞；定位於邊緣運算讓工業電腦
不再只是資料收集設備，而是真正具備「看得見、聽得懂、想得出判斷」的能力，能在工廠端即時完成視覺檢測與設備
健康判讀；更關鍵的是，這種結合正在實際解決⻑期困擾製造業的問題，那就是「經驗無法複製」，透過 AI 學習資深
工匠的判斷邏輯，工業電腦得以把隱性知識轉為可執行的數位規則，即使在技術人力短缺的情況下，生產品質依然能被
穩定維持。 
工業電腦的世代演進

資料來源 : Google，玉山投顧整理、編製 
⾃從NVIDIA執行⻑黃仁勳宣告 「每⼀家工業公司都將成為機器人公司」，並將實體AI定義為繼生成式AI之後的下⼀波科
技浪潮後，AI便不再只是雲端的虛擬智慧，而是能感知環境、理解物理規律、並在真實世界採取行動的能力，這句話所
指向的核⼼硬體，正是工業電腦。未來工業電腦由於內建NPU、GPU等AI加速模組，將可在本地端即時分析影像、震動
等高頻資料，避免等待回傳結果，具有即時推論的效能；另外，也具備低延遲響應且降低頻寬需求，再加上模型的持續
優化，讓工業電腦扮演邊緣AI推論平台，除了整合機器視覺系統的算力核⼼，同時也協作機器人與人形機器人的控制大
腦。邊緣運算正在讓工業電腦從「事後記錄者」轉變為「即時預警者」，工業電腦不再只是設備，而會成為對抗全球勞
動力短缺、突破職人經驗瓶頸的關鍵工具，推動智慧製造從「改善」走向真正的「質變」，預估全球邊緣運算的市場規
模於2033年將可突破千億美元，年均複合成⻑率將可達21%。

工業電腦(IPC)3.0於2026年後將進入主體AI時代

資料來源 : 樺漢法說會資料 
工業電腦歷經成⻑及轉型升級 ， IPC 1.0 (2011至2015年)是工業電腦時代 ， 主要定義讓設備具備運算能力 。 IPC 2.0 (2016
至2020年)是工業物聯網時代，主要是讓設備彼此連結。而IPC 3.0 (2020至2025年)為智慧平台時代，目地是讓數據產生
價值。2026年之後進入主體AI時代，將讓AI從數據分析走向實際執行，從協助決策走向⾃主行動。

## Document 168: 6467泰合 永豐

Source PDF: all report pdf/6467泰合 永豐.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

個股聚焦  
2026/10/20

陳奕均 fion.chen@sinopac.com

泰合 (6467 TT) 
開花結果在即

永豐觀點 
抗血栓口溶膜貼片 BEQIA 歐美取證在即。 
 投資評價與建議 
投資建議買進：泰合 10/20 上市掛牌，掛牌價105.6 元。由於(1) 泰合開發之口溶
膜劑型藥物有效解決高齡與吞嚥困難之臨床痛點下，市場競爭力足；(2) 抗血栓口
溶膜貼片 BEQIA 歐美取證在即，2027 年在授權與上市權利金開始認列下 ，營運轉
虧為盈且呈大跳躍狀；(3) 由於多數學名藥廠受限專利訴訟，最快需至 2028 年 4
月後才可上市銷售，促使泰合享有約一年的「黃金市場空窗期」 ，能與原廠直接瓜
分市場。且因 BEQIA 屬 505(b)(2)新劑型新藥，即便2Q28 後學名藥陸續進場，其
獨特的劑型優勢仍能築起價格防護牆，所面臨的價折壓力相對輕微，建議買進，目
標價 170 元( 18 X 2027 EPS )。

營運現況與分析 
公司簡介：泰合生技成立於 2010 年，專注開發 505(b)(2)新劑型新藥與特殊學名
藥，核心技術為自主研發的 TDS 透皮／透黏膜傳輸平台，將現有口服藥品成分改
造為透皮與透黏膜藥物傳導劑型 ，提供方便使用且更安全的新劑型藥物。 產品開發
上，505(b)(2)部分，治療抗血栓口溶膜貼片(每日兩片)BEQIA 靜待歐美取證中外，
其餘則仍在臨床階段中，困難學名藥部分，化療止吐口溶膜2018 年日本取證銷售
中外，其餘四項藥品則在進行洽談授權合作。 
圖一：泰合產品線開發時程

資料來源：泰合；永豐投顧研究部整理，Oct. 2026

### Page 2

2

口溶膜劑型藥物能有效解決高齡與吞嚥困難之臨床痛點 ：傳統口服錠劑藥物必須經
過食道、進入胃腸道消化，再經由肝臟代謝後才進入血液循環，泰合開發之口溶膜
劑型藥物， 可讓藥膜在接觸舌頭或口腔黏膜數秒內快速溶解 ， 病患完全不需飲水即
可完成給藥， 且部份藥物分子能直接透過口腔黏膜上皮細胞微血管吸收， 直接進入
全身血液循環， 不僅能提高藥物生體可用率， 還能減少胃酸對藥物的破壞與對胃腸
道的刺激下，有效解決高齡與吞嚥困難之臨床痛點，提高患者服藥順從性。 
抗血栓口溶膜貼片 BEQIA 歐美取證在即，提前卡位下，營運動能可期：泰合自主
開發的抗血栓口溶膜新劑型新藥 BEQIA， 預計4Q26 取得歐盟藥證 、2Q27 獲美國
FDA 核准上市。目前該藥原廠在美國市場年銷售額>200 億美元，相較原廠口服錠
劑，泰合 BEQIA 具備三大競爭優勢：不需飲水（服藥便利）、解決吞嚥困難（減
輕照顧端壓力），以及攜帶與給藥便利（優化長照與醫療機構給藥流程）。 
市場競爭方面，由於多數學名藥廠受限專利訴訟，最快需至2028 年 4 月後才可上
市銷售，促使泰合享有約一年的「黃金市場空窗期」 ，能與原廠直接瓜分市場。此
外，由於BEQIA 屬 505(b)(2)新劑型新藥，即便2Q28 後學名藥陸續進場，其獨特
的劑型優勢仍能築起價格防護牆，所面臨的折價壓力應相對輕微。 
泰合海外市場營銷預計將採授權分潤模式，研究部預估 1H27 歐美地區簽約金應
有機會各達 500~1000 萬美金外，隨美國地區 2Q27 上市、通路鋪設及醫保陸續
准入，Peak sales 市佔率 2-3%(預期將落於 2029-30 年)、銷售分潤 12-15%估算
下，稅後 EPS 貢獻將落於 10-20 元。 
財務預估：預估泰合 2026 年營收 0.1 億元，稅後虧損 1.89 億元，稅後 EPS 以掛
牌後股本 5.99 億元估算，稅後 EPS-3.15 元，2027 年預估歐美地區授權簽約金各
500 萬美金及 1000 萬美金 ， 及上市銷售後權利金認列下 ，預估營收將跳升至7.75
億元，稅後淨利 5.68 億元，稅後 EPS 9.49 元。

Editorial 
A Fed credibility-induced bounce  
Strategy summary:  The Fed’s unanimous decision to hike 
rates this week for the first time since 2023 , and pencil in 
another hike by December, confirms our expectations – flagged 
in the last two weeklies - of a central bank willing to establish its 
independence against political pressure. The Fed’s moderately 
hawkish pivot is helping restore its inflation-fighting credentials. 
The BoJ also hiked rates this week. It is the first time on record 
all three major central banks – Fed, ECB and BoJ – have hiked 
in the same month , as they respond primarily to a resurgence 
in energy-driven inflation amid tight job markets.  
We expect the Fed to hike once more by year-end to 4.0-4.25% 
and follow up with another hike in H1 2027 to 4.25 -4.5%. With 
the revised estimates, we revise up our US government bond 
yield forecasts. Nevertheless, with Fed policy uncertainty 
easing, we see an opportunity to use the equity market dip to 
add exposure amid solid corporate earnings.  
Fed moves to restore credibility:  The Fed’s policy pivot this 
week followed Chair Warsh’s hawkish Jackson Hole speech 
and recent economic data prints, namely: a pick -up in job 
creation and inflation over the summer and a sharp rebound in 
oil prices due to the re -escalation of the Middle E ast conflict. 
Given the unanimous Fed decision to hike rates this week, we 
expect two more hikes by  June 2027 on the back of  robust 
growth amid strong AI investments and a tight labour market, 
keeping inflation elevated. We expect this pivot to help the Fed 
rebuild credibility as an inflation fighter.  
Policy outlook less aggressive than market pricing : The 
updated Fed policy rate estimates are lower compared with 
money market pricing of three more rate hikes by June 2027 
since we expect disinflation to take hold by Q2 2027 as the 
impact of oil prices and tariffs fade. We believe policy rates can 
return to the 4.0–4.25% range by end-2027, with the possibility 
of cuts in H2 2027 as inflation pressures ease.  
Upgrading near-term US rates and USD estimates : Based 
on these revised estimates, we also upgrade our 3-month target 
for the US 10 -year government bond yield to 5.0 -5.25%. We 
also revise up our 12 -month target for the US 10 -year bond 
yield to 4.75-5.0% (see page 4).  
Buying the equity dip as focus turns to fundamentals: Fed 
hikes typically do not hurt equities over the medium-term when 
accompanied by strong earnings growth. We estimate a 25bps 
rise in Fed rates (the discount rate) would impact the S&P500 
index and global equities by 3 -4%. However, we expect the 
headwind from higher rates to be offset by strong earnings 
growth, driving positive performance for global equities.  
Furthermore, a hawkish Fed is largely priced in and we expect 
the Fed to deliver fewer rate hikes than currently priced by 
markets. Meanwhile, we believe concerns over AI industry 
leaders slowing the pace of development are overdone. Hence, 
we maintain our preference for a diversified group of large cap 
technology and semiconductor industry leaders (see page 5-6). 
BoJ hikes; modestly bullish JPY : The BoJ hiked rates by 
25bps to a three-decade high of 1.25%, as expected, but the 7-
2 split vote led to renewed JPY weakness on concerns it may 
slow the pace of further hikes. We remain constructive on JPY 
amid narrowing rate differential vs peers as the BoJ potentially 
hikes rates by 25bps once per quarter until Q2 2027 to curb 
rising domestic wage-driven inflation pressure . Any sign of BoJ 
slowing the pace of hikes could lead to renewed JPY weakness, 
fuelling inflationary pressures (see page 7).  
BoE holds; rangebound GBP: The BoE held rates this week, 
bucking the hiking trend among peers, as weakening UK job 
and housing markets offset rebound in energy-driven inflation. 
We expect BoE to deliver fewer rate hikes than the 100bps of 
increase currently priced by markets  as the job market slows 
further. This is likely to keep GBP rangebound (see page 7).  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya

### Page 4

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 18 September 2026

Top client questions 
  Where do you see US government bond  yields following 
the Fed’s 16 September decision to hike interest rates? 
Our view: The US 10-year government bond yield is likely to 
retest the 5.00-5.25% range in the near term. We favour 3-7-year 
maturity bond s in t he US, preferring credit over government 
bonds. We are looking for an opportunity to extend duration 
approaching Q2 2027. 
Rationale: Over the next few months, we expect 2- and 30-year US 
government bond yields to remain rangebound, but the 10-year yield 
is expected to rebound up to the 5.00-5.25% range. The reasons for 
this include: 1) oil prices staying elevated and potentially rise further 
due to the fluid geopolitical situation in the Middle East, with Saudi 
Arabia’s East-West oil pipeline shut, the Houthis now firmly in control 
of the Strait of Bab-al-Mandab, the Strait of Hormuz still closed and 
China returning as a buyer of crude oil in the market ; 2) despite 
notable AI leaders expressing the need to slow AI development, AI 
capex plans are showing no signs of slowing down, which continues 
to drive above-trend growth in the economy. This, in turn, also means 
AI-related heavy bond issuance  is expected to continue. These 
issuers typically issue long-dated tenors, and this competition for 
capital will also exert upward pressure on the US 10-year yields. 
We note that the US 10-year bond yield has room to move lower, 
driven by short-covering activities in the very near term as net-short 
positions had moved to extreme levels before th is week’s  Fed 
meeting. However, we think such downshifts would be short-lived 
and the central tendency for the 10-year yield would be to move back 
up to the 5.00-5.25% range. 
Meanwhile, we see US 30-year yields continuing to reflect the dire 
fiscal outlook for the US government. We expect US 2-year yields 
to continue trading in a range that reflects expectations of further 
federal funds rate hikes. For the record, we only expect two more 
rate hikes from the Fed into the middle of 2027 , while money 
markets are pricing in three more for the same period. 
Going into H2 2027, we expect oil prices to moderate (either 
through a resolution of the M iddle East conflict or a re-routing of oil 
supply chains)  and the inflationary effects from tariffs to fade. 
Inflation by then is likely be close to – if not at – the Fed’s target of 
2%. At this point, we see room for the Fed to contemplate rate 
cuts. US 2-year yields (the tenor that is most sensitive to Fed policy 
changes) will likely start to price th is in. With oil prices and inflation 
cooling, 10-year yields would also have room to come down. 
US 30-year yields, on the other hand, are expected to  remain 
elevated as the US fiscal deficit remains substantial next year and 
could become much worse than its already dire state today. As a 
result, the ‘term premium ’ may keep the ultra -long end elevated. 
Short of some unorthodox policy to artificially cap them lower, 30 -
year yields could continue to drift higher into H2 2027. 
We continue to favour the 3-7-year segment of the yield curve and 
prefer credit over government bond s. We see an opportunity for 
us to extend duration closer to Q2 2027. 
—  Jonathan Liang, CFA, CIO for Fixed Income and FX

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 18 September 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Will the step -up in the  Fed’s risk-free rate trigger a re -
pricing of equities? 
Our view: We expect strong corporate earnings growth to offset 
the headwind of higher rates , driving positive performance for 
global equities. We remain Overweight global equities. 
Rationale: The Fed’s latest 25bps rate hike had been anticipated 
by the bond market, partly reflected in the bond yield  rise we have 
seen so far in 2026. Using the US 10-year government bond yield as 
a proxy for the risk -free rate, the yield has risen by 80-85bps this 
year. We estimate that each 25bps rise in the discount rate presents 
a valuation drag on global equities. On this basis, the rise in yields 
represents a potential 10-14% headwind for equities this year. 
However, the Fed’s hike reflects a solid expansion of US economic 
activity, resilient domestic spending and robust capital investment. 
This is consistent with the positive earnings revisions we have seen 
this year, with  2026 earnings growth projections now at 34% (and 
16% for 2027). Global equities are up 14% year-to-date (YTD), but 
even with a 14% valuation headwind from higher yields, we believe 
they can rise further, given strong earnings growth in 2026 and 2027. 
—  Fook Hien Yap, Senior Investment Strategist 
  What is the outlook for the US 10-year government bond 
yield? What is your tactical view on the DXY? 
Our view: We raise our three-month US 10 -year government 
bond yield forecast to the 5.00-5.25% range. Meanwhile, we see 
a near-term upward bias in the DXY after its recent downtrend, 
supported by recent Fed tightening and higher  short-term US 
bond yields.  
Rationale: The Fed 's decision  to raise the target range for the 
federal funds rate by 25bps to 3.75-4.00% validates the upside policy 
risks we flagged recently, following Fed Chair Warsh's Jackson Hole 
speech and a run of firmer US economic data. We expect growth 
to remain robust, the labour market  to remain tight and inflation to 
stay elevated, supporting one more hike before year-end 2026 and 
potentially another in H1 2027  – fewer than the three additional 
hikes currently priced in by money markets . For the US 10-year 
government bond yield, further policy tightening and lingering US 
fiscal concerns should keep yields elevated, but the Fed’s visible 
response to inflation should limit additional upward pressure, leaving 
the 10-year yield holding around the 5.00-5.25% range. 
Crucially, Warsh provided no forward guidance on further rate 
hikes. Markets had already priced in substantial additional rate hikes 
ahead of the Fed meeting, reducing the scope for further USD upside 
purely from rate repricing. Meanwhile, we expect further policy 
tightening from the ECB and the BoJ, while the RBA also remains 
biased towards further tightening. This should gradually narrow the 
US rate advantage and limit the extent of a DXY rebound. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Anthony Naab, CFA, Investment Strategist

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 18 September 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  How should investors position in technology as AI lab 
CEOs call for a slowdown in frontier AI model development? 
Our view: We believe concerns over “pacing the frontier” are 
overdone. We maintain our preference to remain invested in 
and diversified across Big Tech and semiconductor leaders. 
Rationale: We believe it is prudent for investors to avoid 
overreacting to the recent calls for a slowdown in frontier AI model 
development, as they focus on responsible AI development, rather 
than halting AI model development or reducing spending. The global 
AI leadership  race, reinforced by the US and China’s  strategic 
ambitions, also makes a coordinated slowdown unlikely. 
While the near-term narrative may shift from AI training beneficiaries 
towards inference plays, hyperscalers continue to signal strong AI 
infrastructure commitments and improving monetisation, supporting 
our AI capex outlook. Rising AI adoption and agentic use cases 
should sustain inference demand, even if training activity moderates, 
benefiting custom AI chips, NAND flash memory and hyperscalers.  
We believe the decline in valuations  this year more than 
compensates for any downside risk to AI capex. Our Opportunistic 
idea on Global Semiconductors further reflects our conviction, with 
attractive valuations supporting selective additions during volatility. 
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist 
  With ASEAN and UK equities outperforming AI-focused 
markets in H2 2026, is it worth adding to these markets? 
Our view: ASEAN and UK equities offer portfolio diversification, 
but we are Underweight both markets in favour of US and Asia 
ex-Japan (AxJ) equities, given their stronger earnings potential. 
Rationale: The ASEAN and UK markets have outperformed the 
MSCI ACWI over the past three months, but both have 
underperformed on a YTD basis. Financials is the largest sector in 
the ASEAN (53%) and the UK (28%) and provides steady earnings. 
However, both markets have minimal exposure to the technology 
sector (just 3% and 1% , respectively), ultimately dragging down 
growth. Hence, while defensive markets offer value by increasing 
diversification and reducing volatility in a portfolio, we maintain our 
Underweight allocation for ASEAN and the UK, as we see much 
stronger earnings potential elsewhere in the US and AxJ markets.  
We remain Overweight US and AxJ, supported by robust earnings 
growth, as  AI still has the potential to become a major economic 
growth driver . Our 12-month S&P500 Index forecast of 8,400  
implies low-teens upside, supported by a strong earnings backdrop 
of 25-30% earnings-per-share growth for 2026 and 15 -20% for 
2027. AxJ also boasts the strongest earnings growth among major 
markets, fuelled by the AI semiconductor super-cycle. 
—  Cindy Lam, CFA, Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 7

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 18 September 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Why is it still appropriate to have a positive stance on US 
high-yield (HY) bonds? 
Our view: We remain opportunistically bullish on US HY bonds. 
Lower sensitivity to interest rate volatility, a below-trend default 
rate and a maturity wall pushed out to 2028-29 drive this view. 
Rationale: Recent interest rate volatility has highlighted a key 
structural advantage of US HY bonds: their higher coupon rates  
and shorter tenors relative to Developed Market (DM) government 
bonds and US investment-grade (IG) corporate bonds translate into 
a much lower sensitivity to interest rates, cushioning bond prices 
during yield backup episodes, where bond yields rise sharply. 
YTD, US HY has delivered 1.8% in total returns, outperforming DM 
government bonds and US IG corporate bonds by over 3% each on 
a relative basis. This outperformance was particularly evident during 
the recent back-up in bond yields, as inflation concerns re-emerged 
after renewed Middle East tensions – an environment where HY's 
lower rate sensitivity provided a relative cushion  even as broader 
fixed income markets came under pressure in absolute terms. On 
the fundamentals side, US HY corporate credit quality remains 
resilient. Default rates continue to track below long-run trend levels, 
and the maturity wall has been pushed out to 2028 -29, reducing 
near-term refinancing risk. Against a non -recessionary macro 
backdrop, we expect these credit fundamentals to hold, supporting 
the case for continued opportunistic exposure to the asset class.  
—  Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist 
  Do the latest BoJ and BoE meetings alter your view on the 
central banks’ rate paths and their corresponding currencies? 
Our view: We remain constructive on the JPY and expect two 
BoJ rate hikes this year and potentially one hike per quarter in 
H1 2027 as policy normalisation continues. Meanwhile, the BoE 
held rates at 3.75%. We expect GBP/USD to stay rangebound 
within 1.32-1.36 over the next 1-3 months, with downside risk 
towards the lower end of our medium-term 1.30-1.36 range. 
Rationale: Persistent inflation, higher energy prices and earlier JPY 
weakness support further BoJ policy normalisation . Continued 
hiking should progressively narrow the US -Japan rate gap, reduce 
the attractiveness of JPY -funded carry trades and support  the JPY 
over the medium term towards the 153-155 range. However, with a 
September hike largely priced  in, future tightening ’s pace  will be 
more important for the JPY. Meanwhile, the BoE was split 6-3 in its 
decision to hold rates at 3.75% , with three members preferring a 
25bps hike, signalling elevated inflation risks. However, a weaker 
labour market is likely to limit the pace of BoE tightening. The US-
UK rate divergence is expected to favour the USD over the next 1-3 
months. Further BoE tightening (if inflation remains elevated ) could 
narrow the rate gap and provide support to the GBP later in the cycle. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist

We expect US HY bonds to continue delivering 
strong performance in the near term

Topics  Loss‑making firms are on the rise in China's growth sectors

In China, the number of companies operating in the red is increasing. And the rise is particularly striking in growth 
sectors. One factor behind the increase is the intense competition among firms, which has led to a situation in which 
only the strongest thrive. Another is that many next‑generation growth companies, despite being viewed as having great 
potential, are losing money.

■ Loss‑making firms are on the rise in growth sectors 
In recent years, the number of companies in China's 
growth sectors that are operating in the red has 
increased markedly. The share of loss-making firms in 
all listed firms (below, "loss‑making‑firm ratio") rose 
sharply from 9.6% in 2019 to 26.9% in 2025. Looking 
at the industry distribution of loss‑making firms, from 
2019 to 2022, the ratio increased mainly in 
non‑manufacturing sectors such as real estate and 
accommodation and eating/drinking services, 
reflecting the property market downturn and strict 
stay‑at‑home measures implemented to curb the spread 
of COVID‑19. Later, from 2022 to 2025, the 
loss‑making-firm ratio surged in 
information/communications equipment and 
electronic parts/devices; general, production, and 
commercial machinery; electrical machine ry; and 
chemicals. These four sectors alone accounted for 
around 60% of the overall increase. 
They are the principal growth sectors in Chinese 
manufacturing. This is evidenced by the fact that the 
growth rates of value added in all four sectors exceed 
the average for Chinese manufacturing. Many of the 
high‑tech manufacturing industries that the Chi nese 
government has prioritized for development are 
included in these sectors. For example, 5G base 
stations and semiconductors fall under 
information/communications equipment and 
electronic parts/devices. And robots fall under general, 
production, and com mercial machinery. Meanwhile, 
solar cells and batteries fall under electrical machinery, 
and biopharmaceuticals and organic chemical 
materials fall under chemicals.  
Looking at recent developments in the 
manufacturing of information/communications 
equipment and electronic parts/devices, the global 
communications standard began shifting from 4G 
(LTE) to 5G services in around 2019, and ChatGPT was released in November 2022. These developments 
have provided tailwinds for the growth of the sector. Recently, as it has become clear that ordinary 
consumers and companies can make use of AI services on a daily basis, demand for AI‑related devices and 
semiconductors, as well as d ata centers, has soared. In this environment, many firms are focusing on 
strengthening development, manufacturing, and sales, and the sector's value added has continued to 
increase, rising from 780 billion yuan in 2022 to 910 billion yuan in 2025. Yet over  the same period, the 
number of loss‑making firms increased from 113 to 198, and the loss‑making‑firm ratio jumped from 17.2% 
to 30.1%. 
■ Competition among companies means only the strongest thrive 
One reason loss‑making firms are increasing despite operating in growth sectors is the intense 
competition among companies. The fight for market share has led to overheated investment competition

### Page 3

ASIA MONTHLY REPORT  October 2026, No.307

The Japan Research Institute, Limited

2 
and price competition. In information/communications 
equipment and electronic parts/devices manufacturing, 
the rate of growth of non -current assets from 2022 to 
2025 (+35.4%) far exceeded the growth rate of value 
added (+16.9%). This indicates that supply capacity is 
expanding faster than demand. In addition, the 
consumer price index (CPI) increased by just 0.4% 
over the same period. Service prices rose 2.2%, 
reflecting wage increases, while goods prices fell 0.7% 
due to excess supply in manufacturing. 
Information/communications equipment prices 
decreased by 3.5%, larger than the drop for goods 
overall. 
As the selection of the fittest through competition 
among companies has progressed, profits have become 
concentrated at a small number of highly competitive 
firms. The share of profits earned by the top 1% of 
firms in information/communications equipment and electronic parts/devices manufacturing rose sharply 
from 24.0% in 2022 to 43.3% in 2025. Leading companies are simultaneously establishing positions as 
both "domestic champions" and "global champions."  
■ Next‑generation growth companies are viewed as having great potential even though they 
are losing money 
However, loss‑making firms cannot simply be lumped together and labeled as losers. Globally, investors 
have shown increasing interest recently in companies with high growth potential even if they are operating 
in the red, and China has a considerable number of such firms. 
In information/communications equipment and 
electronic parts/devices manufacturing, one out of 
every two loss‑making firms is a company attracting 
strong investor interest. The Shanghai Composite 
Index rose 28.5% from 3,089 points at the beginning of 
2023 to 3,969 points at the end of 2025. 20 of the 198 
loss‑making firms in 2025 were not yet listed in 2023, 
and if these are excluded, there were 178 companies 
operating in the red as of 2025. Of these 178 firms, 96 
(53.9%) saw stock‑price increases over the period 
2023-2025 that exceeded the rise in the Shanghai 
Composite Index.  
One characteristic of these 96 companies with strong 
relative stock‑price performance is their active 
investment in R&D and intangible assets. Their 
average R&D intensity (R&D investment / sales) in 
2022 was 9.6%, and their ratio of intangible assets to 
value added stood at 60.5%. Intangible assets include 
computer software, patents, copyrights, and 
relationships with customers and suppliers. In contrast, 
the 82 loss‑making firms with weak relative 
stock‑price performance had an R&D intensity of only 
6.5% and an intangible‑asset‑to‑value‑added ratio of just 38.9%. 
As the above has shown, the increase in loss‑making firms in China's growth sectors is notable, and the 
factors behind it include not only the selection of the fittest as a result of competition among companies but 
also the emergence of next‑generation growth firms that are viewed as having great potential. 
  (Shinichi Seki)

Topics  Climate change heightens the risk of growth stagnation in Asia

Climate change could elevate the risk of economic growth flatlining in the ASEAN zone and India by causing labor 
productivity growth and capital accumulation to stall. To mitigate or avert such a scenario, establishing a business 
environment and institutions that promote "adaptation investment" by both the public and private sectors is an urgent 
task.

■ Heavy‑rain and flood damage is becoming increasingly serious in Asia 
Huge downpours and floods 
resulting in human casualties have 
been a frequent occurrence in Asia in 
the past several years. Still fresh in the 
memory is the debris flow that 
occurred in August this year near the 
border between Nepal and China, 
which as of t he time of writing has 
been recognized as a major disaster 
with thousands dead or missing. And 
in November of last year, Southeast 
Asia and South Asia saw 
record‑breaking torrential rains, 
resulting in numerous casualties, 
particularly in Indonesia and Sri  
Lanka. 
One factor that heightens the risks 
of such floods and heavy rains is 
climate change. As temperatures rise, 
the amount of water vapor in the 
atmosphere increases. Therefore, 
global warming could intensify 
torrential rainfall. Although such risk 
is shared a cross Asia, the way its 
effects are felt differs by region. 
Within Asia, the ASEAN zone and 
India are heavily affected by 
monsoons (seasonal winds), with 
many areas experiencing concentrated 
rainfall during the wet season. In such 
areas, warming can furthe r intensify 
monsoon‑driven heavy rains, 
increasing the risks of floods and 
landslides.  
■ Climate change will delay the escape from the "middle‑income trap" 
Climate change not only results in human casualties by way of natural disasters, but may also undermine 
the long‑term economic growth potential of ASEAN and India through various channels. The Asian 
Development Bank (ADB) [2024] conducted a quantitative an alysis of the extent to which climate change 
will weigh on Asian economies over the long term. Based on the IPCC's "very high GHG emissions" 
scenario (SSP5‑8.5), the analysis assumes a 4.7°C rise in the Earth's average temperature by 2100 relative 
to pre-industrial levels, and estimates the difference in real GDP between that and a hypothetical scenario 
without climate change. The results illustrate the long‑term impact on economic growth through declines 
in labor productivity, stagnation in capital accumulation, and downw ard pressure on consumption due to 
sea‑level rise, floods, heat waves, and other phenomena caused by climate change.  
An examination of the results reveals that in every country/territory, the pushdown effect on real GDP 
relative to the no‑climate‑change scenario expands over time, with 2035, 2050, and 2070 each seeing 
greater impact. The pushdown effect for ASEAN and Ind ia is −15.0% to −8.8% in 2050 and −30.2% to

4 
−18.1% in 2070, larger than the effects for China (2050: −4.8%, 2070: −10.3%) and Central Asian countries 
(2050: −1.7%, 2070: −3.8%). Within Asia, the ASEAN zone and India can be considered regions where the 
risk of economic stagnation due to climate change is relatively large.  
This may make it even more difficult for ASEAN countries and India to escape the "middle‑income trap." 
Although their income levels have risen, they still remain far from high‑income status. Indonesia, Malaysia, 
the Philippines, and Thailand became middle‑income countries in the 1990s or earlier, but have still not 
been able to join the groups of high‑income countries. Furthermore, Vietnam and India, which became 
middle‑income countries in the 2000s or later, have yet to enter a high‑growth trajectory like China once 
did. Climate change, by causing labor productivity growth to stall amid rising temperatures and companies 
to curtail investment due to more frequent disasters, may lead to income growth in the ASEAN zone and 
India being delayed further.  
■ Improving the environment to accelerate "adaptation investment" will be important 
To alleviate the pushdown effect of climate change on growth, expansion of "adaptation investment" by 
the public and private sectors is essential. Specifically, this could include improving working environments 
and promoting measures to lighten workloads a nd reduce headcounts in order to offset declines in labor 
productivity caused by rising temperatures, as well as developing infrastructure and strengthening facilities 
to prevent damage to production equipment from sea‑level rise and floods. However, ASEAN  and India 
cannot be said to have sufficiently 
developed the foundations for public 
and private promotion of such 
adaptation investment.  
As part of its Global Adaptation 
Initiative (ND‑GAIN), the University of 
Notre Dame (U.S.) evaluates countries' 
ability to use investment to achieve 
climate‑change adaptation in the form of 
a "readiness" score with three 
components: economic readiness, 
governance readiness, and social 
readiness. Respectively, these 
components assess 1) whether a 
business environment conducive to 
adaptation investment by private -sector 
companies has been put in place, 2) 
whether the effectiveness of institutions

and policies to support private-sector adaptation investment has been secured, and 3) whether tangible and 
intangible social infrastructure that promotes investment has been established. 
Looking at readiness in 2024, ASEAN countries and India generally rank in the middle to lower tiers 
globally, with a large gap between them and developed countries. Notably, the Philippines, India, and 
Thailand fall outside the top 100, indicating weak momentum for adaptation investment. Breaking down 
the scores, problems are especially evident in the economic and governance categories. India and Thailand 
have particularly low eco nomic readiness, ranking 113th and 124th, respectively, and five countries 
(Malaysia not included) fall outside the top 100 for governance readiness. Given this situation, to accelerate 
private‑sector adaptation investment, India and Thailand in particular  need to move swiftly to improve the 
financial environment to make it easier to procure necessary funds domestically and internationally, relax 
regulations to encourage the launch of new businesses, and reform labor markets to enable the smooth 
securing of human resources. In addition, for Asia as a whole, it will also be important to boost confidence 
in the institutional factors that enhance application of investment for adaptation by increasing policy 
stability, ensuring the effectiveness of legal systems, and curbing corruption.

(Tomohiro Hosoi)

## Document 298: GLOBAL MARKET CALL_ Downgrading 'Go Global' To Market Weight

Source PDF: all report pdf/GLOBAL MARKET CALL_ Downgrading 'Go Global' To Market Weight.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: GLOBAL MARKET CALL: Downgrading 'Go Global' To Market Weight
Date:    3 hours ago
GLOBAL MARKET CALL: Downgrading 'Go Global' To Market Weight

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
GLOBAL MARKET CALL:
Downgrading 'Go Global' To
Market Weight
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 18 Oct 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Robert Nyman / Unsplash
We are downgrading the “Go Global” portfolio strategy from overweight to
market weight and raising “Stay Home” (in the US) from underweight to
market weight.
Overseas earnings remain impressive, but the rallies in emerging market
economies’ stock markets have been led by just a handful of AI-related
stocks. Last week's rout in South Korea showed how vulnerable such
concentration leaves these stock markets. Additionally, European

economies look set to struggle as France's fiscal problems loom, and
China's economy depends almost entirely on exports. Plus, the Middle
East war could flare up again before or after the US midterm elections on
November 3, keeping oil prices, inflation, and interest rates elevated.
Here's more:
I. Global Bonds
The global bond rout took a breather last week. Yields fell in all eight
major bond markets we track, led by the US (chart). The US 10-year

Treasury yield fell 16bps to 5.08%, even as the CPI rose 3.7% y/y and the
PPI jumped 6.3% in September. That suggests bond investors see these
rates as attractive relative to their long-term inflation expectations.
The spread between French and German 10-year yields has stabilized at
around 137bps, near its recent high (chart). Spreads for Italy, Greece,

### Page 3

Spain, and Portugal have eased slightly from their late-September peaks.
Contagion from France has eased for now. But France's own fiscal risk
premium hasn't. The National Assembly began debating France's 2027
budget on October 13. Meanwhile, Paris is burning!
II. Global Stocks
Brazil leads the country ETF rankings mtd, up 12.3% in dollars, ahead of
next Sunday's presidential runoff (chart). South Korea is at the bottom,
down 17.7%. The US is up 0.1%, ahead of the All Country World ex-US

ETF, down 1.9%.

### Page 4

Europe's share of the All Country World MSCI's market cap has fallen to
13.0%, from as high as 30.8% in 2007 (chart). It looks set to keep losing
ground for a while. The US MSCI’s share has firmed to 64.8%, up from
62.4% in March. The Emerging Markets MSCI’s share has held at 12.1%
despite the Korean selloff.
The ratio of the US MSCI to the All Country World ex-US MSCI has turned
up in recent weeks (chart). It remains below its early-2025 high.

### Page 5

III. Earnings & Valuation
The All Country World ex US MSCI's forward earnings is up 41.4% y/y,
while forward revenues is up 11.0% (chart). The global economy is doing
well.
Overseas, earnings have far outpaced stock prices this year. The All
Country World ex-US MSCI is up 10.5% ytd, while its forward earnings is
up 35.9% (chart). Its forward P/E has dropped 18.9% ytd.

### Page 6

The US MSCI's forward P/E is 19.1 versus 12.2 for the All Country World
ex-US, a 57% premium (chart). US profit margins justify about half of it.
The US forward profit margin is 16.8%, about a third higher than the All
Country World ex-US's 12.7%.
IV. China Focus
China remains a source of deflation for the global economy through its
massive trade surplus. Exports jumped 36.3% y/y in September to a
record high (chart). The trade surplus was $133.5 billion. Semiconductor

exports more than doubled during the first nine months of the year.
Booming exports are the only reason China's economy isn't imploding.

### Page 7

The domestic economy remains weak, weighed down by a rapidly aging
population and a property debacle. The Shenzhen Real Estate Stock
Price Index is testing a floor that has held since 2012 (chart). We would
worry if it broke below that level.
The stock market hasn't benefited from the export boom either, so Beijing
appears to be propping it up again. Regulators met on Thursday to
discuss stabilization measures, according to Bloomberg. They reportedly

urged companies and state funds to buy shares and asked some
investors to curb selling. Mainland A-shares, where Beijing's state-backed
funds do most of their buying, held up into mid-2026 before turning down
(chart). Offshore Chinese stocks have been falling since late 2025.

### Page 8

clause.

### Page 1

• The global landscape remains complex, shaped by AI, 
energy prices and tariffs. 
• Taiwan’s AI cycle remains strong, with robust AI 
exports and investment and an intact semiconductor 
competitive moat. 
• AI is generating broader domestic spillovers, with 
wealth effects and wage growth supporting 
consumption. 
• Taiwan is cushioning the energy shock by diversifying 
energy imports and absorbing much of the increase in 
upstream costs. 
• AI-driven growth is likely to moderate from 4Q26 into 
2027, as hyperscaler  cash-flow pressures rise and 
higher global rates lift investment costs. 
• Energy remains a n inflation risk, with domestic 
electricity prices facing upward pressure after the 
November local elections. 
• GDP growth is expected to moderate to high -single 
digits from 4Q26, while we maintain our 11.6%/5.6% 
GDP forecasts for 2026/27. 
• Inflation is likely to remain above 2% in 4Q26, with our 
2.0%/2.1% CPI forecasts for 2026/27 unchanged. 
• We maintain our call for CBC to hike 12.5bp to 2.125% 
in December, amid tighter liquidity conditions. 
• Housing should recover gradually despite credit 
easing, as excess new -home inventory remains a n 
overhang.

Taiwan: AI, energy and 4Q outlook

13 October 2026

### Page 2

Taiwan: AI, energy and 4Q outlook October 13, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            2

Global backdrop

The global landscape remains complex, 
shaped by the interplay of AI, energy 
prices and tariffs, with significant 
implications for growth, inflation and 
financial conditions.

1) AI

The debate over the durability of the AI 
investment boom continues.  Tailwinds 
include technological advances, broader 
adoption, and s trategic and geopolitical 
considerations. Headwinds range from 
uncertainty over investment returns  and 
hyperscaler financing pressure to 
physical bottlenecks , safety concerns, 
and regulatory uncertainty.

Hyperscaler financing pressure has 
attracted increasing attention . In the 
early stages of the AI investment boom, 
hyperscaler capex was largely financed 
by internal cash flow. This is now 
changing, as free cash flow is expected 
to turn negative from 4Q, requiring major 
players to rely on debt issuance and 
other external financing increasingly.

At the same time, higher global interest 
rates are raising the cost of AI 
investment. The Fed resumed rate hikes 
at its September meeting, while global 
bond yields remain under upward 
pressure amid persistent inflation and 
elevated public and private borro wing 
needs. Our baseline assumes US rates 
will rise by 50bps this year and a further 
25bps in 2027 to 4.50%, with global 
long-term rates also remaining under 
pressure. This could increasingly weigh 
on the economics and pace of AI 
investment.

US-China AI competition is also drawing 
increasing attention.  Across the five 
layers of the AI ecosystem, China is 
making rapid progress in energy, 
infrastructure, AI models and 
applications, supported by its large scale, 
cost-effective domestic manufacturing 
supply chains and huge consumer 
market, which provides a wide range of 
real-world scenarios for AI adoption. The 
emergence of a more integrated and 
cost-competitive Chinese AI ecosystem 
could increasingly challe nge the US 
ecosystem, which currently operates at a 
relatively higher cost.

China, however, remains relatively weak 
in semiconductors.  Around half of 
China's domestic AI chip market still 
relies on foreign suppliers, including 
lower-end NVIDIA chips. This 
semiconductor bottleneck remains a key 
constraint on China's AI development. In 
our view, Taiwanese semiconductor 
companies' competiti ve moat therefore 
remains largely intact in the near term.

semiconductor sales remained strong 
through 3Q, while WSTS expects growth 
to moderate from 90% this year to 27 % 
in 2027.

2) Energy

The US -Iran conflict remains at a 
standoff, keeping the energy -price 
outlook highly uncertain.  New 
developments are likely after the US 
midterm elections in November, with 
both further escalation and de -
escalation possible. Crude oil prices have 
remained elevated at around 
USD100/bbl amid a persistent 
geopolitical risk premium . In contrast, 
refined product prices have risen more 
sharply as refining disruptions and 
tighter product logistics have amplified 
the supply shock. Natural gas prices 
have also increased sharply, reflecting 
disruptions to LNG supply and limited 
alternative shipping routes.

Taiwan has been actively diversifying 
its energy import sources to cushion the 
impact. LNG imports from Qatar have 
fallen to zero since April, while imports 
from the US and other suppliers have 
surged to offset the shortfall, helping 
maintain stable domestic electricity 
supply.

3) Tariffs

US tariff actions are likely to accelerate 
after the US midterm elections in 
November. Two major tariff measures 
remain in the pipeline. The Section 301 
forced-labor tariff was implemented in 
July, while final tariff action under the 
Section 301 overcapacity investigation, 
which covers 16 economies, remains 
pending. Meanwhile, Phase 1 of the 
Section 232 semiconductor tariffs was 
implemented in January, while Phase 2, 
covering a broader range of 
semiconductor-related products, 
remains pending.

Taiwan has secured a relatively 
favourable framework through its trade 
and investment agreements with the 
US. Taiwan signed an Agreement on 
Reciprocal Trade (ART) and an 
Investment MOU with the US earlier this 
year. The ART establishes a 15% US tariff 
rate on Taiwanese goods, while the MOU 
provides Taiwan with preferential 
treatment under Section 232, including 
for semiconductors. The ART has yet to 
enter into force, and it remains to be 
seen how the final Section 301 tariff 
burden will interact with the 15% ART 
framework. Meanwhile, major Taiwanese 
companies such as TSMC are expanding 
their US investments under the 
Investment MOU, which could support 
relatively favourable treatment for 
Taiwan when broader Section 232 
semiconductor tariffs are introduced.

Taiwan 4Q outlook

Taiwan has navigated the complex 
global environment relatively well so far 
this year, supported by robust AI exports 
and investment, diversified energy 
imports, and efforts to absorb higher 
upstream energy costs. Some 
normalisation is likely in 4Q26 –2027, as 
the AI -driven growth boost moderates 
and domestic energy prices face greater 
pressure to adjust.

1) Growth

GDP growth remains broadly on track 
with our expectations. High -frequency 
indicators suggest that GDP growth 
should remain around 12% yoy in 3Q, 
broadly in line with 12.9% in 2Q but below 
the 15.4% peak in 1Q. Growth is expected 
to moderate to a high -single-digit pace 
from 4Q onwards. We maintain our full -
year GDP growth forecasts at 11.6% for 
2026 and 5.6% for 2027.

The AI cycle remains the key driver of 
Taiwan's growth.  Information and 
communication products and electronic 
components accounted for nearly 80% 
of total exports in the first nine months 
of this year, while manufacturing 
accounts for nearly 50% of gross fixed

capital formation. From 4Q onwards, 
export growth is expected to moderate 
from exceptionally strong levels to a 
more normal but still robust pace, 
broadly following the trajectory of 
global hyperscaler capex and 
semiconductor sales. Investment is likely 
to follow a similar path.

For now, Taiwanese technology 
companies continue to report robust 
revenue growth, led by semiconductors, 
ODMs/EMS, and PCs and servers. 
Capacity utilisation at leading Taiwanese 
foundries has also continued to rise, 
suggesting that investment demand 
remains strong.

The AI boom is also beginning to 
generate broader spillovers to the 
domestic economy. The AI-driven equity 
rally has generated significant wealth 
effects, with the TAIEX up 70% year-to-
date and Taiwan becoming the world's 
fifth-largest stock market. Taiwanese 
households have benefited from the 
appreciation of financial assets, with 
equities and mutual funds accounting for 
nearly 30% of total household financial 
assets. The equity rally has also 
generated positive income effects. Wage 
growth has picked up, led by the services 
sector, particularly financial services, as 
strong earnings at financial 
institutions—especially securities 
firms—have prompted higher 
compensation to retain talent. These 
wealth and income gains should support 
household consumption, creating a 
positive feedback loop that broadens the 
benefits of the AI boom across the wider 
economy.

2) Inflation

CPI inflation is also tracking broadly in 
line with our forecast. Headline CPI 
remained elevated at 2.4% yoy in 3Q, up 
from 1.2% in 1Q and 2.2% in 2Q. We 
maintain our forecast for headline CPI 
inflation to remain close to 2.5% in 4Q, 
and retain our full-year forecasts at 2.0% 
for 2026 and 2.1% for 2027.

Energy inflation remains the main 
upside risk.  The government has 
absorbed much of the increase in energy 
costs through state -owned CPC and 
Taipower, limiting the pass -through to 
domestic consumers. With losses at both 
companies widening, the government 
will need to decide whether to provide 
additional subsidies to cover the losses 
or allow further increases in domestic 
energy prices. The September re view 
postponed the adjustment to the 
December meeting, when the overall 
situation will be reassessed after the 
November local elections. Taipower's  
latest review indicated that electricity 
rates would need to rise by 12.8%. A hike 
in electricity prices could lift inflation 
expectations and generate second -
round effects, particularly as the labour 
market tightens and wage pressures 
increase.

3) Monetary policy

The central bank kept the policy 
discount rate unchanged at 2.00% at its 
September meeting, in line with our 
expectations. We continue to expect a 
modest 12.5bp rate hike at the 
December meeting, bringing the policy 
rate to 2.125%.  By then, CPI inflation is 
likely to have remained above 2% for 
around six consecutive months. The 
possibility of an increase in electricity 
prices following the November local 
elections could further lift inflation 
expectations, strengthening the case for 
a December rate hike.

Financial conditions have already 
tightened despite the absence of a 
policy rate hike.  M2 growth remained 
around 7% yoy in 3Q, well below double-
digit nominal GDP growth. At the same 
time, bank loan growth has accelerated 
to more than 10% yoy, driven by strong
